Action! — Pay-per-view

Players and Pawns 2003, 30 November 2003

30 November 2003
Content noticeA period document, served unedited. It carries the humor, language, and content typical of its era and community — some of it crude, dated, or offensive, including material that wouldn't be written today. See Curation.
Players and Pawns 2003 Safeco Field — Seattle, Washington ---- Max Danger vs. Deft Rules | Strategies | Pawns | Game Be a Player. Writer: Roland. Cue: “Weak and Powerless”, Perfect Circle. Tonight… eight men enter the wrestling ring with a dream. A dream that began when they first became wrestlers. A dream to become the top man in the industry. A dream to become the greatest fan favorite of all time… Or, in some cases… the greatest heel. Tonight eight of those men have this opportunity. They have a chance to become Action’s next Joey Malone… Syphon Fission… or LLB… Tonight could be step one… step one of maybe even passing their greatness. The Player’s Tournament promises success. The Player’s Tournament will grant that success. But there is only room for one… not eight. Tonight, with hard work… with determination and will, one man will succeed. One man will have a chance to achieve their dream. To headline a show. To become a top star. A chance to become Action’s Player. And the rest of them? They are pawns. Pawns controlled by the player. The man who is at the top of his game. Start the clock… when these three hours are done, this game is over. And there will be just one winner. One man who stands as Action’s Player. Tilling my own grave to keep me level Jam another dragon down the hole Digging to the rhythm and the echo of a solitary siren One that pushes me along and leaves me so Desperate and Ravenous I’m so weak and powerless over you Someone feed the monkey while I dig in search of China White as Dracula as I approach the bottom Desperate and Ravenous I’m so weak and powerless over you Little angel go away Come again some other day Devil has my ear today I’ll never hear a word you say He promised I would find a little solace And some piece of mind Whatever just as long as I don’t feel so Desperate and Ravenous I’m so weak and powerless over you Desperate and Ravenous I’m so weak and powerless over you “Weak and Powerless” by A Perfect Circle. The Pay-Per-View theme song to Players and Pawns 2003. The promo changed to Safeco Fields as fireworks went off in front of the entrance. The Fans all stood, cheering and waving their signs as they did. Some of them read: “DANGER OWNS!” ; “I Write Better Columns Than KSZ!” ; “Look, My Name is Roland Erman Too!” and “I LOVE YOU CORAL AVALON!” to name a few. The Pay-Per-View theme played throughout the PA system… as the scene then scanned the entrance, showing the layout. A ramp-less walkdown, with a castle-like entrance for the wrestlers to come out of, as above it, stood a massive Elongation (possibly the biggest to date - stretching across the entire area), which was also surrounded by the castle-like entrance as it seemed to cover the whole entrance side. In front of the entrance were two giant chess pieces. Made of wood, each of them were about ten feet wide. To the right (the left side if you were to walk out from the entrance) was a giant rook. It was the color red… and stood about twenty feet in the air. To the left, was a giant pawn. It was gray and stood about ten feet in the air. The scene then went to the ring. The canvas was not done in a normal run-of-the-mill light blue mat like Action and the WWE had always done, though. Instead, the canvas was in the design of a chess board, as shown here… with black turnbuckle padding and dark gray ropes... along with aprons that read “Player or Pawn?” on it. The camera changed one last time, as the fireworks and “Weak and Powerless” died down, to show two men sitting behind the English announce table. A straight line in the design of a chess board stretched across the bottom of the screen, as a giant brown rook twirled on the left-hand side of it, bringing up the text that read “Liam Ump & Derik Shaw”. They introduced themselves and the Pay-Per-View… and the show was ready to go. ACTION WRESTLING PRESENTS PLAYERS AND PAWNS Safeco Field in Seattle, Washington 10/30/03 Max Danger vs. Deft Referee: Benny Doyle. Writers: Michael K. D. & Joe S. The setting sun shined it's ending light upon the castle in centerfield of Safeco Field in Seattle, Washington. Yes, there was a castle, which was very castle-like in its castle-ness, standing tall, wide, and ominious. From the mouth of it was the long aisle that stretched all the way to the ring. On the sides of the entrance were a 20-foot tall Rook and a ten-foot tall Pawn. Since it was the start of the show, the fans were going crazy. The smoke from the pyro was slowly making its way up to and out of the ceiling, as the show's theme song played throughout. All the people had their signs up as the cameras panned around. The Action!Tron lit up with the graphic PLAYERS TOURNAMENT, FIRST ROUND. The crowd reacted more positively towards it. That's right, Players & Pawns was going to kick off with a little tournament action. It was going to be the winner of the last tournament, the King of Submission, Max Danger versus Deft. (The Action! Championship Round Robin Tournament is not included. That was a whole different beast altogether.) But before that happened, Max Danger's visage appeared on the Action!Tron to the cheers of tens of thousands. "Max Danger, tonight," was the words that escaped James Attwood's mouth as the camera pulled back to show Danger standing in front of a Players & Pawns checkerboard backdrop with Jessica to his left and behind him, and James to his right and in front of him. The fans cheered more loudly. "You are going to be facing off against Deft in your first round match. In the last tournament, you defeated Jeff Garvin to become the King of Submission. How do you feel about your chances tonight?" "Oh, the memories I have of beating Jeff," Max said looking upwards as if imagining it all over again. The fans roared in an uproarious fashion. They, too, loved that incident. "But my night isn't about Jeff, because we all know he's going to lose to Coral tonight anyway. He has that one well under control." More cheers. "No, my night is about this tournament. To be crowned Action! Wrestling's Player. And, hopefully, vault myself into contention for the Action! Championship." For those keeping score at home, Max wasn't in his usual attire. He was wearing black and blue, although his entire look was different. Jessica's too. If you would just kindly head over to his and her profile pages, shimmy on down to the bio section, then you'll find some links to what exactly the Dangers were wearing. But that was really unsubstantial. It mattered very little what they were wearing, aside from it signaling a slight change in character. Anyway... "I'm about to head out there now to face Deft in the first round," continued Max. "And I feel that my chances are good. Afterall, I have the experience in tournaments, having won one already. Being a former holder of three different championships gives you some benefits going into something like this as well." "On Pressure Point, Deft had a few comments about the tournament," Attwood said. "Did you happen to hear those?" The Danger Man nodded, "I did. And..." "'And' what, Max Danger?" The camera, at first, was hesitant to pan out of it's selected view, with no backdrop being provided. But eventually, the boys in the truck decided that, well, good TV is too good to pass up. "What do you think of what I said... Max Danger?" Danger looked at Deft momentarily. He put a small smile on his face and looked away for a second, sort of laughing to himself for being put in one of those situations where you're talking about someone and they are right there, and they get all uppity about it. Or something. Max said, simply enough, "Well, I think you were wrong." James quickly passed the microphone back underneath Deft's mouth as he let out, "Oh?" And he cocked his head slightly, indicating that he wanted the King of Submission to elaborate. Again, Max looked away, this time down at his new boots. When he lifted his head, he had that same half-smile on his face. "Yeah," he started. "I don't believe this is your tournament at all. One good match with Coral Avalon does not a "Player" make, Ed." The former holder of the Dyad, Carnage, and Bantam Titles shook his head slightly, "You've been away for, what is it, a month? I hardly think that's the fault of anyone but yourself. So, while you were at home, nursing your wounds or what have you, I was here, competing night in and night out. Busting my back, literally, just for the opportunity to one day strap that Action! Championship around my waist. And with the recent losses of my prized possessions - the Carnage and Bantam titles - well, this tournament... my tournament is the stepping stone to that." Max put his hands on his hips and locked eyes through his opponent's mask. That lasted for a second before Max lifted his arms with his palms out and pointed at Deft. Just one of those hand gesture things. "So, I'm sorry, but this isn't your tournament. It's mine." The fans reacted well to Danger's words. They agreed. This was his tournament. It was his... destiny. Who else could possibly win it? He was, afterall, the one who brought three outfits for each round. He had it in his mind that he was going to win, and there was not going to be a single person that could stop him. Simple. As. That. Deft's eyes retreated, it looked like, inside his head. Most people don't react well to confrontation. Max Danger was exactly the opposite. "The masses aren't always right, Max Danger. Tonight, all this, it doesn't belong to you. Preach the Gospel of Danger to them, out there. Because I don't care about your accolades. I don't care about you busting your ass. I don't care that you, Max Danger, are the King of Submission." Deft checked the strings tying his headpiece on, making sure that they were tight to go. "All I care about is taking what's mine." As Deft was about to walk past Max, to head out to the ring, Danger grabbed him by the bicep, holding him back. James Attwood held the microphone up over his head to get it down between the two men. Jessica had backed around to the otherside of her husband, up against the backdrop. Squeezing his arm, the Danger Man said, "Then I'm sorry that there is nothing out there that belongs to you... besides an early exit." "Max Danger, I will say this once. Let FUCKING go." The King of Submission merely smirked and let go of Deft's arm. After a slight staredown, Deft faced forward and marched away, leaving Danger to stand and watch him go. Max turned to his wife. She took his hand and the two of them walked off leaving James Attwood alone in front of the Players & Pawns backdrop, "They're coming your way!" And the Action!Tron flickered from the small interview area into the Deft video as "One of Them" by Jurassic 5 & JuJu began to boom throughout Safeco Field. The fans were already pretty much on their feet, with their eyes locked on the castled-entrance. Sadly there was no moat full of alligators and/or crocodiles - depending on which ones you like best... or both - that certain people could fall into. Anyway, despite that disappointment, they gave Deft a little pop when he walked through the entrance. He was the only other person competing in the Players Tournament that seemed to really want to win it, besides Max Danger. That's what made this matchup so interesting. They both wanted it, seemingly, more than anyone else. Which was the tragedy of the tournament booking, as they had to face each other right off the bat. Such was life. Deft stopped inbetween the giant rook and pawn peices, and then looked out at the over-stuffed building, just completely packed to the gills with people. It was unbelievable. It was one of the biggest, if not the biggest, crowds Deft had ever competed in front of. He took a deep breath and started down the long, long aisle that lead into the squared circle. The same thought swirled around in Edward Estrada's head, "This is my tournament. Mine." He believed it completely. All the way down the aisle, he played with the fans. Worked them into as big of a frenzy as he could. Slapped hands with a few people along the way. He stopped just as he stepped onto the protective matting and listened to Pat Hoskins calling out his name. Up on the Action!Tron, his stats were being shown to all of the people along with Derik Shaw's "Points To Victory" notes about what Deft needed to do in order to triumph over the Danger Man. Number one on Shaw's list, "Hire Wrestling 101." Oh, that was hilarious. Deft didn't notice those things, however. He just took another deep breath, keeping his mind and body in tuned with the job at hand before jumping up onto the apron. He stepped through the ropes and then lifted a finger high into the air, bringing up some more cheers from the crowd. It wasn't the reaction that his opponent would receive or anything. But it was nice. A few boos were mixed in, because, well, you go against the Danger Man, you're gonna get some boos. It was inevitable. As Deft walked around the ring, he slapped his abs along the "rite the wrong" tattoo he proudly wore in honor of his mother. Benny Doyle checked him for any international objects, but found none. Eddy Estrada walked over to his corner, where he pulled on the ropes, stretching as he waited. He pulled on them twice before standing upright and hopping on his feet. After hopping he turned his head side to side, loosening it. And just as his music faded out he adjusted his mask. Making sure it was securely in place. Because his opponent was about to emerge from the castle. FUCKIN' POSERS! N.E.R.D. hit the speakers with "Rock Star", and it echoed inside the home of the Seattle Mariners. Not a single person remained seated as the name MAX DANGER appeared across the Action!Tron. From inside the castle came the prince and princess of Action! Wrestling, Max and Jessica Danger. The two of them stopped between the two chess pieces just as Deft had, and looked out among the people. Danger's long flowing sleeveless Akiyama-style coat waved behind him as he and Jessica started down the aisle. The screams of the masses were unreal. Deft was lucky he wore a mask, as it drowned out the shrieks enough for him to keept his one though in mind. "This is my tournament. Mine." Black and blue was the color of choice as mentioned earlier. Which seemed highly appropriate given the state of the Danger Man's back. He and his wife were very methodical in their pace down the aisle. It was odd seeing such a crowd favorite completely ignore the out-stretched hands of his adoring fans. But he had always done that, and it wasn't going to change anytime soon. He would say things to them. Play to them to rile them up and get them to cheer even louder than they already were. Maybe not as much as most would do, but enough to throw them into a frenzy. Pat Hoskins called out Danger's name as they reached ringside, bringing out more screams from the Seattle faithful. Deft remained standing in his corner across the ring. Keeping his mind focused. Just waiting for his opposition to enter the ring so that they could get this thing started. Ordinarily, Max would hold his wife's hand and guide her up the ring steps and across the ring apron. Instead, she walked up the steps as Danger walked around the ring. He got went up the other ring steps behind Deft, who had backed turned around to watch. Max did the Chris Jericho high-step across the apron, causing the fans to cheer more loudly than before. He didn't do the spin on the apron though. He just remained standing and looking out at the people, just as Jessica did on the opposite side of the ring. After casting looks towards one another, the two simultaneously entered the ring and met in the center. After a shared moment of looking out at all the people, Max went towards the ropes and climbed onto them, putting one foot on the middle rope and the other on the top, lifting his arms high into the air. Bad back or not, this was a big event, and he wasn't about to skimp on the entrance. Besides, it was making Deft stew more and more. He had thought himself to be disrespected by the Danger Man just before the match. As if he wasn't a threat to Max's chances of winning the tournament. Pish-posh, Deft thought. There was no doubt he was going to beat Max, and then beat two more people to win the whole thing. His mind was dead set on that goal. Finally, Danger's entrance music died away and referee Benjamin Francis Doyle brought the two men to the center of the ring. He went over his usual pre-match instructions, making sure to point out that he wouldn't be using his count out, as he never did, but wouldn't hesitate to disqualify either men if he got out of control. After the barely make-out-able instructions from the Irish-speaking Doyle, he checked Max for possible objects of the international flavor. None were found in his much-too-big elbow pad, his knee pads or shinguards. He was weapon free... if you didn't count his mastery of all things submission... or wrestling in general. That was just as dangerous a weapon as any weapon. Besides the fist-wrapped chain. KSZ could attest to that. Seeing that Max had no weapons, Deft headed back to his corner to stretch and make his final preparations for this match. It was a big one. Possibly the biggest in his career? It was his biggest opportunity, that was for sure. While no one was certain what exactly winning this tournament would provide, it certainly was going to be a major accomplishment if someone was able to get past three men to claim the cup, or trophy as it were. As, yes, there was a trophy for the thing. It was being guarded like the Stanley Cup. Inside a large trunk case with three armed guards. No one had seen the thing yet. But they all knew there was one. Max still had his King of Submission trophy inside a box at his home in Augusta, Maine. All his old wrestling stuff was still packed in boxes, sitting in the old ring in their basement. Anyway, as Deft did his thing in the corner, Max did his as well. He and Jessica shared a small kiss on the lips, drawing some hoots from the crowd, because they were all horny, sex-deprived fans who got off on such simply things as that. Danger also removed his wedding ring and handed it to his wife. She placed it into her pocket, gave her husband another kiss, and then stepped through the ropes. Her low-cut jeans forced her pinkish-reddish underpants to be slightly visible, making the men and boys(and some girls) cheer loudly. They booed when she pulled her pants back up. She just smiled. Danger, also, had taken off his coat and handed it down to his wife. Benny Doyle had rung the bell. The sound of it echoed through the building and out into the open air that the opened roof provided. The sun was slowly fading away, as darkness was biting at the heels. It desperately wanted to bring coldness and darkness to the proceedings. It was lucky that Action! Wrestling had chosen the field with the retractable roof. Everyone just hoped that it wouldn't fail to close when the day became night and they were all sitting in the cold night. That would not be fun for anyone, unless they were a member of the Polar Club. The crowd came alive as the two men stepped towards the center of the ring. There wasn't going to be a big staredown, although that was always nice for those who brought cameras to the building. Nope, they just immediately went into circling each other. For about the fourth consecutive week, Danger actually had a size advantage on someone. He was enjoying it just a little, as it was a twice change of pace from always being the little guy. The two men were circling each other. Deft had reached forward, dropping down onto one knee, searching for Max's leg, but he had stepped back to prevent it. Deft bounced right back up onto his feet and continued with the circling process. Finally after testing each other, the two men locked up. Max was very quick to pull Deft's arm back into a hammerlock. The Danger Man was well aware of Deft's shoulders. They would be his focus. And everyone knew about Danger's back. But it was never about knowing Max's weaknesses. Everyone and their grandmother knew about them. It was a matter of finding your opportunity. Max didn't exactly leave too many openings, so you had to make sure you could pounce on them when they arose. Meanwhile, Deft attempted to counter the hammerlock, but ducking down and going behind Max, but as Deft was doing that, Danger spun to his left and ended up right behind Deft, still pulling his left arm in a hammerlock. That didn't sit well with Eddy, so he tried it again, but Max was too quick and spun right back into position. Obviously another tactic was going to have to be used to get out of the situation. Reaching back, Estrada had ahold of Danger's hair. Benny was warning Deft against pulling his hair and was about to start his five-count before Deft let go of it, ducked down again, but instead of going behind Max, he locked his free arm around Max's waist and attempted to lift him up. Danger was blocking as he still had ahold of Deft's left arm. So, he spun outward again, where he was standing face-to-face with Deft while holding his arm out between them. The King of Submission quickly locked his arms around the arm, putting his clasped together hands at Deft's shoulder with Deft's outstretched arm across his own shoulder. Max added a couple of kicks to Eddy's legs, knocking him down onto one, while applying more downward pressure by stepping closer and pulling the arm in a more uncomfortable angle. The masked wrestler quickly disregarded Doyle's questions of submitting, and did a forward roll. Deft kicked Max in the side of the head and kipped up. With his arm freed after the kick, Deft grabbed Danger by the arm and whipped him into the ropes. The New Agenda, as he had dubbed himself on the final Pressure Point before this Pay Per View, was looking for a hiptoss on the Danger Man, but Max had blocked the attempt. He quickly wrapped his arm around Deft's head, and spun with him until he was ready to drop Deft with a neckbreaker. Instead, Max went for an inverted snapmare type of move, flipping Deft over his head and in front of him. He kicked Edward in the stomach and shot him to the ropes. The Danger Man went for his own hiptoss, but it, too was blocked. Deft smacked Danger in the stomach to double him over before placing his leg behind Max's head and flipping backwards. He quickly went to clothesline Danger to the mat, but Max caught his arm in a sidearm takedown, and attempted to apply the crippler crossface! The fans were going wild! So early in the match, and Max had found his first major opening for a submission. Deft was ducking his head out of the crossface, not letting Danger lock it in. And with his arm free from being trapped between Max's legs, he was able to scoot away. He got into the ropes, which forced Benny Doyle to start a count. Max kept fighting for Chris Benoit's primary finisher until the count of five, at which point he rolled away and got onto his feet. He didn't waste time, although the fans were clapping politely like it was an indy event. He went right at Deft and kicked him in the stomach as Deft got onto his feet. He grabbed Deft's arm and looked to be trying an irish whip. Estrada, however, countered, but Max had reversed that into an attempted back body drop, but much like Danger did in his imitation of Ultimo Dragon, Deft cartwheeled or flipped out of the move. He added a hard kick into Max's back, which sent him stumbled forward and into the ropes. Danger spun around on his heels. Deft had run across the ring and hit the ropes for more speed and momentum. Danger charged towards Deft, too. Both men had evasion on their mind, as Max slid across the mat and Deft dove over him and into a forward roll. Deft was up much quicker than Max and grabbed his arm as he was getting up. The former Carnage Champion was sent into the corner. Deft ran towards him and jumped up onto him, looking for a monkey flip. Danger, blocked however, grabbing Deft's feet and flipping him backwards. Eddy landed on his feet. Max charged out of the corner looking to clothesline the bejesus out of Deft, but the man trained by Duel ducked underneath it. Danger had stopped cold and turned around just as Deft had. Deft kicked Max in the stomach first, though, before taking him by the head and slamming it into the top turnbuckle. From there, Deft lifted Max up onto the top turnbuckle, but was quickly kicked directly in the face, knocking him backwards. Danger dropped down off the apron, but was met with dropkick, that knocked him through the ropes and to the floor. He shook his head as he leaned against the apron. Jessica shouted out, "Watch out!" Danger looked up and saw that Deft was trying to hit him with a baseball slide, but thanks to Jessica's warning, Max was able to take a step backwards to avoid it. When Deft's feet touched down on the floor and he turned around, he noticed that the King of Submission wasn't on the ground or leaning against the guardrail like he had hoped. Instead, he was slamming a hard elbow smash into Deft's mask-covered face. He added a chop to get the entirety of Safeco Field to go, "WHOOOOOOO!" before rolling his opposition back into the ring. Danger climbed up onto the apron, as Deft was getting up onto his feet. As Max stepped through the ropes, Deft was running to the adjacent ropes. Danger stepped all the way into the ropes and was hit with a shoulder block from Eddy - which probably wasn't the wisest of ideas - knocking him down onto his back. Deft quickly grabbed Max by the hair and brought him up onto his feet. Deft gave Max a couple of forearm shivers before backing him against the ropes. Deft attempted an irish whip, but Danger reversed the momentum, but instead of using the whip on Deft, he pulled the masked man's shoulder right into his own, and quickly pulled Deft's arm back into a hammerlock. Yeah, that was frustrating. While you couldn't see that on Deft's face, you could just tell. Grabbing the back of his tights, Danger charged towards the corner with Deft and slammed his shoulder into the middle turnbuckle. Danger reached down and grabbed Eddy by the mask and pulled him back up. After bringing Deft's other arm into a hammerlock, he did the exact same thing, slamming the shoulder into the turnbuckle. With Deft leaning against the turnbuckles, Max spread his legs apart and sat down between them. He grabbed both of Deft's wrists and pulled his arms back before placing his feet right on the back of Deft's shoulders. Pressing forward with his feet and pulling back on the arms well... just listen to Eddy Estrada's screams. That's how painful that was. And luckily for Max, Deft wasn't touch the ropes. Benny Doyle was standing to the side and leaning over to check, holding onto the ropes and balancing himself on one leg. He also asked Deft if he wanted to give up. Eddy shook his head and said, "I can't!" See, this was his tournament. He was going to win it. There simply was no other answer he could give considering all that. Deft was just thinking about how badly he was going to hurt Max once he got free of his predicament. Of course, he had no solutions as to how he was going to go about doing that, but he liked to think further ahead than most. So, once he happened to be free, Max should just watch out. So, despite Deft's previous answer to the questions, the Irishman still had to ask whether the Duel trained wrestler wanted to give up or not. And Deft still let Benjamin know that there wasn't any possible way that he could submit, and thus Max released Deft's arms, but still pushed forward with his legs, sending Deft's face crashing into the middle turnbuckle. Luckily his mask provided a little padding for such things. The King of Submission got up onto his feet before reaching down and grabbed Deft by his mask. He pulled Eddy out of the corner and up onto his feet. Max grabbed Eddy by the waist and lifted him up onto the top turnbuckle. Unfortunately, the moment Deft's feet touched the middle turnbuckle, he clamped his arm down around Max's head, and pushed off. Tornado DDT? Well, it would have been had Max not blocked. Instead, Deft was kneed hard in the stomach when Max ripped his head free of the not-tight-as-he-would-have-liked front face lock. With Deft doubled over, Max pulled Deft's arm back into a hammerlock again, and raced forward towards the corner. Deft's shoulder was slammed into the turnbuckle hard. Taking Deft by the mask, Max pulled him out of the corner and slammed a hard elbow smash into his face. Deft fired back with a kick, but it wasn't enough to do any damage, as Max came right back with another elbow smash. He tooked Eddy by the head and snapmared him over. With Deft sitting on his ass, Max kicked him hard across the shoulders. Another great thing about working the shoulders? Wasn't going to be so easy for Deft to lift his shoulders when he couldn't muster the strength to kick out. Anyway, Danger walked around to the front of Deft and brought him up with the combination of pulling on his arm and his head. Danger brought Deft into a front face lock and casually threw Deft's arm over his head and held onto the side of his tights. The vertical suplex was flipped out of by the masked wrestler. As Deft landed behind Max, he locked in a waistlock, but his arms weren't quite free of their pains yet, so Danger was able to pry Deft's arms apart. He held onto the left wrist with both hands and yanked on it, flipped Estrada over onto his back, trying to seperate the arm from the rest of Deft's body. Danger quickly dropped a leg across the shoulder, before getting back up and placing the arm behind his knee and dropping down, trapping it underneath his leg. Deft wasn't feeling too hot at the moment. Danger freed Deft's arm and stood up. He brought Eddy up onto his feet as well. Max went for a high kick to Deft's shoulder, but he blocked it with his forwards and countered with a leg sweep Ultimo Dragon-style, knocking Max onto his back. The King of Submission wasn't down for long, and he stood with a smirk on his face. "Finally come to join me in this match, have you?" He shook his head. "About damn time." Deft simply kicked Max in the chest, although he didn't like kicking much at all, it was the best option at that point. Danger was staggered back a couple of steps before receiving a straight knee into the stomach. Eddy Estrada whispered, "Oh, Max Danger, I'm taking over this match now." And he went for an irish whip. Max, however, reversed it and sent Deft to the ropes. Upon his return, which was met with flowers, gifts, and a potential tilt-a-whir... nope, Deft countered whatever Max wanted to do with a spinning headscissors. The fans were getting more into it now, thanks to the quicker pace. Not ALL fans are going to sit through mat-wrestling, and while this Seattle crowd appreciated it, it wasn't what they wanted out of an opening bout. Following the headscissors, Danger got up fairly quickly and ran right into a hurricanrana from Deft. He hooked Max's legs for the cover. One. Benny could only strike the mat once before Danger had used his superior leg strength against Deft's weakened arms to pull him over in a pin of his own. It received the same count as Deft's, as the masked man rolled out. As he was on his knees and still bent over, Max quickly shot up in a seated position and locked Deft's arm behind his body in a double underhook. Deft had his feet under him, whereas Max was sitting down, so Eddy was able to fight up onto his feet, pulling Danger up with him. Deft had, in fact, stood upright with Danger still hanging on but completely vertical above him. It was quite the sight, but Danger released the double underhook and dropped to his feet on Deft's side. Eddy Estrada quickly used a side kick to strike Max in the stomach and double him over, since he couldn't really get into close enough range to knee him. From there, Deft took Max's arm and sent him into the cables. An irish whip was hell on the shoulders, too, though no one every really thought it was. But it wasn't something so severe that it would force Deft to never do them. Since, you know, he just did one. Anyway, coming off the ropes, Danger slid between Deft's legs. He stood up with his back to Eddy, and Estrada was quick to dropkick him in it, knocking him through the ropes and right onto the apron. Directly off of the Honor and Glory, Arcade and Son match… the scene went backstage, in front of the interview set up area. A giant chess board stood on its end, as James Attwood held a mic in his hand… but this mic wasn’t a normal one. Instead a black chess piece was in place of it. A king to be exact. Attwood turned to the camera, and then spoke. “What a night it’s been so far!” He said. “The roof is now closed… we’re five matches in this great event… and up next, the new Action Superstar, Anaconda… or, well, should I say older star? The former TWF wrestler is back in the business. Anaconda, tonight you face another rookie to Action in Jason Deline. Any thoughts?” Anaconda stepped into view. He wore a two piece suit (resemblance to Bret Hart’s outfits) in the colors of dark green and light green. “Thoughts… hhmm…” Anaconda pondered. “Well James, I’m not really sure. This will be my second match in Action. I’m hoping to make a strong return to the ring and get a victory.” Attwood nodded. “Obviously Deline will be enthused and maybe even nervous for his first Pay-Per-View… but despite being a veteran, will you be nervous as well?” “I’m not really sure.” Anaconda replied. “I don’t know what to expect from me yet. I faced a brilliant wrestler in Max Danger. I don’t want to take anything away from Deline… but I think I should have a better attempt at winning.” He had heard it - every word of Anaconda’s speech, hyping himself for the upcoming match, but something about it just wasn’t sitting well in the pit of Deline’s stomach. He was getting looked at as a blow off, but he didn’t consider himself that. In fact he believed himself to be far from it. After all Anaconda may have been coming off a loss to ‘The Danger Man’, but Deline had just faced Coral Avalon… he knew he was ready. So, it was time to crash the party… the small bash Anaconda was throwing himself. It meant he was going to have to be on camera though - something that scared him worse then Garvin’s hair, and yes, Garvin’s hair is a scary thing, you’ve all seen it. “Well, no time better then the present.” He said to himself. Deline stepped into view of the camera, his heart felt like it was going to pop out of his chest. He was dressed almost like Triple H, with the black spandex shorts, but being confused with Triple H isn’t something you should do… as Deline’s not all hyped up on roids and he knows how to sell a move. “You know for allowing ‘The Danger Man’ to completely destroy you, you’re in a rather good mood tonight.” Deline began. “I guess I must have missed the memo that said when old guys dress up like faded WWE memories that we were meant to care about what they thought.” Deline took a deep breath, trying to keep his composer. You could tell he was nervous, but Anaconda wasn’t looking well… he wasn’t looking much of anything. Deline was losing his natural adrenalin though. “Well…” Anaconda started. “Max Danger is a pretty talented guy. I’m rusty. I definitely don’t have this match in the bag…” Anaconda looked Deline over. “I guess we’ll have to see.” “Well, at least you’re grounded enough to know that losing to Danger was the least of your worries.” Deline muttered. “Tonight is the night you have something to worry about.” With that… Jason walked off, leaving Anaconda standing there, to the silence of the Crowd. “It’s time to suck the breath out of my first victim…” He said. “Jason.” ---- Anaconda vs. Jason Deline Anaconda vs. Jason Deline Referee: Ryan Watts. It takes a lot of training and a lot of hard work. For the hundreds that actually do wrestle, few actually make it big. James Atwood, in front of the chessboard backdrop was awaiting his upcoming interview. Rook Black. Rook had a towel around his neck, still clothed in his new ring gear. James began, "Rook Black, you've earlier you advanced to the semi-finals over Tanya Isama, and up next you'll be facing none other than your friend Max Danger. What are you feelings on that?" "Feelings." "Well, James, Tanya put out a great match, easily surprising me with her talent tonight. Anyone can appreciate that through watching either of them compete, but I really didn't grasp the totality of Tanya Isama's talent until tonight. She's got to be one of the most gifted people I've been in the ring with, and I think that's saying quite a bit considering the level of skill we see here in A Exclamation Mark Wrestling." "But Max Danger, Rook, he's regarded as perhaps being the most talented pure wrestler in the world today, what do you have to say about him?" "Max is Max, and I've had the opportunity to get to know him a little over the past few months. What separates him from a talent like Tanya is the fact that Max's level of experience completes all the necessary tools that this business requires to be successful." "How do you think you'll fare in that match?" "I've wanted to wrestle Max Danger for a long time now. He's been victorious over our current Champion, Jeff Garvin, he's been victorious over Frank Heimdal, he's been victorious over Impulse Brian James, he's been victorious over Coral Avalon, he's been victorious over Keith Scott Zimmerman, over Sharc, over a lot of people. The only person who is left in this tournament that Max has never beaten would be me. We've never had a match against each other." "So you place a lot weight and importance into this match." "Of course I do. I mean, I place a great deal of importance in every match that I'm in, but this particular match I've wanted for a long time." "How's that hand of yours holding up? Are you concerned that it'll be a target for the King of Submissions." "I'm not concerned about anything like that, James. The only things that I'm concerned about are little things. The small details that add up to change everything. I've been mentally preparing to face Max Danger since Out of Control." With a disarming smile, James Atwood just had to ask, "Now, forgive me for this, but considering you're a chess player, do you think you could pull out an old school promo for us, with all the allegories and metaphors to go with our theme for Players and Pawns?" Rook looked down at his feet holding back some laughter. He took a deep breath, then exhaled, looking up directly at the camera. In a low and rasping voice, Rook began, "In this kind of game, every move is absolutely critical, every choice, every step, every decision could be the one that brings disaster and calamity. This game is thought to be about war, but that's only a small part of it. This game is about everything. This game, this game that we play, brings minds into conflict. Beyond strength, toughness, and technical ability, this game is about patience, this game is about control, and most importantly it's about discipline. Act impulsively, and you will be outmaneuvered. Act cowardly, and you will be dominated. Act conservatively, and your options will vanish. Each piece is representative of that army, that political machine, and every piece is essential to victory. Victory doesn't belong to any particular piece on that board, it belongs the meager as well as the mighty. And when all the strongest plays have been made, and all the exchanges have taken place, it's among the remaining pieces that the final outcome will be decided. When the board has been wiped clean, it's what is left over that makes winners and losers. After Max Danger has given me his best, and I've given him my best, it'll be at that moment that the both of us will have to reach deep down to pull out the last few tricks that we have left. And it's then that the inconsequential, the meager, the insignificant pieces that people contemptuously call pawns that become the most dangerous of weapons. In this type of game, sometimes it's not the strongest move that directly leads to victory, but the weakest. Max Danger is a solid player, perhaps the greatest player. But there is always an opening because every attack is open to a counter attack, every escape is subject to pursuit, every cunning gambit relies on your opponent's gullibility. And when, and it will happen, Max Danger creates an opening, an opening that I've been waiting for, it's then that I'll make my move. At that moment, he will have lost." "How's that work for you, James?" Laughing, James replied, "Fantastic. Thanks for the interview, Rook, best of luck to you." And it was much easier to get a faster worker to slow down than it was to get a methodical competitor to speed things up. It was a problem all the other wrestlers faced when they stepped into the ring with Max, because it was only once in a blue moon that he wanted to just rip things up and go balls to the wall all match long. The two most recent examples would be the Dyad Championship match between the champs, IWC, and the team of Max Danger and Coral Avalon. Also, the steel cage match that saw Danger lose his Bantam Championship to Keith Scott Zimmerman. All non-stop action. This, however, was going to try Rook's patience. It was very fortunate that he was a chess player. A game that required patience. Otherwise, he would become very frustrated with Danger's methods. Max realized all this, but he figured he was a better wrestler than Rook. He could take advantage of the tiniest slip-up. He wasn't sure if Rook could. Sure, he had watched him wrestle, but he hadn't been in the ring with someone like Max before. And the ones that were close, like Daniel Phillips, it was done more in the style that Rook would have wanted. Enough with the jibba jabber™, Mr. T. The two men began to circle one another. Starring: ???. Writer: Paul L. Shadows had filled the caldasack and had fallen upon the parking lot of the arena. The only hint of glimmering lights that shined upon the scene was the blinking light show presented atop the big towers. It shined brightly “Action Wrestling: Pressure Point LIVE!” In a way, one may say it was ‘RATINGS~!’ but one man simply did not care for the lights. This mysterious figure looked up from the ground and stared hypnotically at the Action Wrestling promotion that shined above the world for all to gaze upon. He took a good inhalation of nicotine from his lighted cigarette and puffed out the smoke through the corner of his mouth. The cigarette itself was half ash, dangling on the shaft of the cancer stick by a mere thread (so to speak). He took another inhalation to calm his nerves. It caused the cigarette to give off a slight dull glimmer of ashy light, only to allow the ash to fall to the floor. He reached over, pulling the cigarette from his mouth and tapping the remnants of the ash away. He had been waiting for hours; standing outside the Action Wrestling arena and keeping still beneath the shadows of the parking lot. He had stood since the late afternoon. While others sat happily in their homes, eating their dinners, watching their televisions and living a peaceful life…he was forced into a state of determination, which was driven by the strongest emotion known to human beings. An emotion that can determine what a man does with his life, and an emotion that controls a man’s life. Anger. Rage, vengeance, the need for revenge, the need for self gratification; every single form of anger rose in his body and forced him to remain outside of that arena. He took his eyes away from the media light show and peered over at the backstage entrance garage. The slowly opening door caught his attention. He leaned his head to the side, briefly taking his figure away from the shadows. He inattentively watched as the cars began to pull into the arena. “No…no…no,” with every car that passed into the downward ramp, he whispered to himself the same thing, “No…no…not it” He sighed in frustration, bringing the cigarette back to his mouth and taking another puff of the sweet nicotine. He curled his lip in frustration, as his left eye began to twitch. He wasn’t a patient man, especially when it came to seeking revenge; but in this case, for everything to work properly, he would have to be patient and simply wait for HIM to show up. But again, he found himself in a trance with the sign above him. “Action Wrestling,” he whispered to himself with a hint of a sadistic grin. He looked into the lights, allowing its mesmerizing gleam to shine in his eyes. With a lift of his right hand, he tilted his hand upward and pointed his pointer finger in the center of the Action symbol. He put his thumb up in the air, forming a sideways “L” with his finger, keeping his pointer pointed at the symbol of all he hated. Mixing several different medicines and liquids together. No disqualifications, no countouts, and no time limit. The only exception was if there was any outside interference, which would cause the match to be completely thrown out. And nobody wants that. Any consolation Sabotage would get from their effort tonight would have to wait. Outcome: IWC via Pinfall; Retain D Titles. ---- Sharc vs. Rook Black Sharc vs. Rook Black Referee: Ref Reffington. Writer: John L. And then there were two. In a competition that officially began with eight men trying to become Action’s impact player for the coming year, now the field was narrowed to two men. Rook Black and Sharc. In the next several minutes, both men would be striving to take their careers up to new heights, regardless of who came out victorious. The winner, however, would get a piece of immortality. Beside the ring awaited a trophy to be awarded — still not revealed to the world. Only one man would be given the Player’s trophy. Only one man would have his name inscribed on the trophy as the 2003 winner. The loser’s name would not. Three suited Action officials kept the trunk less than a foot away from them at ringside, waiting on the outcome. Who wanted it more. Rook Black? Sharc? This was their stage now. In Your Dreams You See Me Clear I Have No Restraint Nor Fear VNV Nation's "Dark Angel" brought forth Rook Black. A strobe light kicking it along with the bass, Rook stepped out for the third time tonight, leaned his head back and rolled his shoulders. Black was wearing an Action T-shirt with a rook symbol upon it. Black could have seen this night as fate for him, being named ‘Rook’ and all and his chess-themed offensive moves. His loose fitting pants even had the ‘chess’ theme going of the evening, with one leg white and the other leg black. Give Me Time I Will Be Clear Given Time You'll Understand What Possesses Me To Right What You Have Suffered Black marched to the ring, putting one cowboy boot in front of the other as the crowd cheered him on. I'm In This Mood Because Of Scorn I'm In A Mood For Total War To The Darkened Skies Once More And Ever Onward He'd arrived. Black earlier this evening had defeated Max Danger and Tanya Isama to advance to this spot. He was determined and focused, but yet with that feeling inside that accompanies all gigantic events in life. Excitement and nerves all jumbled together in a giant ball. The fans greeted Black, with at least half, maybe more, hoping he would be the man to come out on top. The pounding drums and heavy opening guitar riff of "Five Minutes Alone" by Pantera roared into life over the speakers. Sharc took several steps out before pausing his entrance to stare down the aisle into the ring where his destiny — and Black — awaited. Neither of these men had worn gold, but victory here tonight could put them onto a path similar to the one Max Danger used last year after winning submission tournament, launching him as a big-time superstar and impact player in Action. Sharc walked that aisle in his usual mid-paced manner, clad in an Action logo T-shirt and his silver leather pants. Earlier tonight, he had defeated Hiro Kitagami and ‘Impulse’ Brian James to advance. He slid under the bottom rope and stared up at Black from his knees before grabbing the ropes and dragging himself to his feet. He walked past Black and raised his arms to the crowd. You've waged a war of nerves But you can't crush the kingdom Can't be what your idols are. Can't leave the scar. You cry for compensation. "HOLY SHIT! HOLY SHIT! HOLY SHIT!" The crowd couldn’t believe it! Now THERE is something you’ve (most likely) never seen on a wrestling pay-per-view before. A wrestler being crotched on a foul pole! Black ended up sitting on the wall when he rebounded from the move, moaning in pain, though his moaning could barely be heard by even himself over the crowd. Was Sharc done there? OH NO!
